## v6.1 The candidate, exactly as Suno recorded it

Read off the song's own record on suno.com (2026-09-13), not from any sheet — **no sheet in this
repo held this prompt**; it was grown by hand in the browser.

- **Model** v6 (`chirp-hawk`) · **created** 2026-09-09 15:00 UTC · **workspace** camping-Jack
- **Style Influence 70 · Weirdness 60** · `aug_creativity 3` (probably Variety — unverified)
- **Style box 1,186 characters.** 🔴 Suno's record holds all 1,186, so we cannot prove the tail
  was cut off — the 2026-09-12 "truncation" diagnosis is a hypothesis, not a finding. What IS
  measured: it asked for 174 BPM and came out at **117.45 BPM**, both times we measured.
- **My Taste:** the take predates the 2026-09-10 retirement, so the account-wide My Taste may have
  been live when it generated; the song record has no field for it. The block Kai saved is at the
  end of this section, as history — **never paste it**.

**What the take actually is** — timings from a per-second loudness scan, contents only where two
independent Gemini runs agreed:

| Time | What happens |
|---|---|
| 0:00–0:20 | intro: a lone repeating low guitar riff over a dark synth drone, no drums |
| 0:20–1:03 | verse 1: dry spoken voice, riff underneath, sparse drums arrive |
| ~1:04 | the music cuts out for the whispered line (the `acapella` cue working) |
| 1:06–1:27 | shouting returns, then drop 1: full drums, distorted wall, screaming lead |
| 1:28–1:45 | verse 2, sparse again, riff underneath |
| 1:46–1:49 | the music cuts out for the second whispered line |
| 1:50–2:11 | shouting section, one short dip at 2:08 |
| 2:12–2:33 | drop 2 — the loudest part of the song |
| 2:34–2:50 | bridge, stripped back |
| 2:52–3:01 | a dip, then one last loud push |
| 3:02–3:20 | outro: riff and drone, one deep sub hit at 3:18 |

**Kept by both listens:** the palm-muted riff is the spine; dry spoken and shouted vocals; the
whisper-then-slam breaks; distorted-wall drops with a screaming lead. **Missing in both:** 174 BPM,
amen rolls, bagpipes, Gregorian chant. **Leaked in:** sung "ooh" phrases despite the bans.
**Disputed:** one voice or two — the listens disagree; settle it by ear at the bridge.

**The words are identical to §4 below** (399 words each, checked 2026-09-13). The take's lyrics box
differs from §4 only in **cues** — most importantly `[whispering voice loosing hope | no music |
acapella]` where §4 has `[change tone to spoken word]`, which is what made the breaks.

```text
Dark UK drum and bass, neurofunk, 174 BPM in a minor key with a steady steppers groove; male 1960s British mod R&B hollers, first gravelly and weathered, second higher and plummy, never sung, bone-dry against reverb-drenched ballad space. A low palm-muted guitar figure repeats beneath every vocal, joined by synthesizer drones and distant Gregorian chant backing; bagpipe tones shadow the minor harmony. First verse begins over a solitary detuned synth texture, then dry chopped breakbeats and amen rolls enter; drops hit with full-weight drums, Reese sub, screaming wavetable lead, tambourine on every beat, and slow wide chiming Britpop guitar walls at half speed, all fused rather than remixed. Verse two flips colder drums and sharper hats while the guitar figure remains alone beneath the voice; the second drop grows heavier, more distorted, with longer amen tears, thicker guitars, and higher lead. Bridge strips back to intimate shared-room vocals, sparse riff, chant haze, and restrained steppers pulse. Keep hard English consonants, throaty unpolished attack, clean BBC diction, flat delivery, no vibrato, and no instrumental vocal gaps where lyrics specify continuous speech.
```

singing, sung verses, sung chorus, melodic vocal, vocal melody, vocal hooks, crooning,   clean sung melody, autotune, harmonies, grime MC, UK drill, road rap, trap, hip hop,   young MC, American accent, American vocal, US rap, transatlantic, southern drawl, ragga   MC, toasting, Jamaican accent, dancehall vocal, soprano, operatic vocals, vibrato,   angelic voices, sustained vocal notes, female vocal, children's choir, choral harmony,   orchestral strings, violins, cello, string section, piano, glockenspiel, brass band,   marching band, oompah, dixieland, guitar solo, lead guitar, shredding, acoustic guitar,   wah, mashup, bootleg, remix, rock remix, nu metal, rap rock, rapcore, grunge, punk, epic   trailer music, reggae, dub, ska, music hall, vaudeville, pantomime, ragtime,   honky-tonk, liquid dnb, jump up, pop, lo-fi, jaunty, playful, whimsical, bouncy,   comedic, novelty, parody, uplifting, major key, double time, tempo change, slow tempo

The My Taste block Kai saved (history only — not pasted, Personalize is off):

```text
Vocals: two English men in their late forties and fifties, talking over the beat rather than singing — there is no melody in either of them. One is low, gravelly, smoke-worn and nasal, close and bone dry on a cheap mic. The other is higher, clean and plummy BBC English, precise and unhurried, talking down at you from a wide room. Talking over singing, every time. Flat and unbothered over theatrical. Raw over polished.

Music: dark UK drum and bass and neurofunk at 174 BPM, minor key. Rolling Reese bass, deep sub, dry chopped breakbeats, industrial stabs. Drum fills that tear across a whole bar every few bars: chopped amen rolls, snares tumbling over each other, loud at the front of the mix, then straight back into the groove. Arrangements that climb in steps: a track that opens on one unaccompanied instrument and does not show its full weight until the drop. Big aggressive wavetable synthesizers where an orchestra would be: screaming detuned leads, hard-synced and formant-morphing, growling gnarly mid-range. In the drops and outro only, a wall of chiming Britpop guitars, layered and doubled, strumming slow and wide at half the speed of the break, tambourine on every beat, so the guitars, the synths and the break are one piece of music, not a remix of one by the other. Under the vocals the guitar is only a low palm-muted figure, never chords, never a tune. No orchestral instruments and no piano anywhere. Cold, grimy, relentless, rock steady in tempo start to finish. A British record, made in Britain, about Britain.

Register: bleak, bitter, angry, played completely straight. Whatever is funny lives in the words alone — the music never winks and never plays along.

Subject: British class, money, work, and who the economy left behind.
```

## v6.38 Round r46 — the liked talking blues, FAST and as dark and aggressive as it goes (2026-09-16)

**Brief, Jack 2026-09-16:** *"camping-r42-talkingblues-v6-w20 can you do a fast talking version of this that is as dark and aggressive as it can be - do very varied versions."* Base: `r42 talkingblues atom` (§v6.34).

**Kept:** the canon words, the two English men (`a weathered working-class bloke and a plummy posh gent`), talking not singing (the singing and poetry-reading bans), American-folk bans, the settings.
**Changed, in every lane:**
- **Fast.** Every lane's Style box leads with speed. `slow tempo, laid-back, gentle, soft, calm, mellow, relaxed, happy, major key, lullaby` added to Exclude.
- **Dark and aggressive through delivery words** (r37 lesson): snarl, spit, bark, hiss, venomous, shouting the punchlines.
- **Lyrics: commas stripped, words unchanged** (checked by script, word for word). A comma is a beat of inserted space, so fewer commas = faster (`lyric-craft.md` "Punctuation is the brake"; the r11 precedent). `?` and `!` kept. Each verse cue carries the lane's delivery.

**Six very varied lanes** (BOLD rule: outside the cast, the longest text any two share is 24 chars):
| Lane | Music | Delivery |
|---|---|---|
| `bare` | one acoustic guitar strummed hard and fast, bone dry, nothing else | rattled off, raw, furious, cracking with rage |
| `punk` | folk punk, a thrashed battered acoustic, distorted, clipping room mic, stamping feet | snarled, hoarse, heckling, half shouted |
| `gothic` | fast minor-key fingerpicking, a low cello drone, boot stomps, claps, cavernous | fast, low, threatening, hissed |
| `industrial` | a distorted acoustic grinding one riff, an industrial kick, clanking metal | barked, every word hammered |
| `train` | a relentless train-beat strum, a slapped upright bass, a stomp box | rapid-fire, jabbing, never pausing for breath |
| `drone` | doom folk, a detuned guitar hammering one chord, a feedback drone, war-drum toms | venomous, shouting punchlines, ragged |

🔴 **Risks:** `punk`/`industrial` may tip into shouted rock singing · fast + rhythmic can turn talking into rapping (our rule: rhythm makes him rap) · `train`'s slapped bass sits in the rockabilly pool, which can come out American.

Settings: v6 · SI 80 · Variety Off · Max Mode ON · Male · Personalize off · no Voice · Auto · camping-Jack · Weirdness **20** and **40**. Titles `camping-r46-tbfast-<lane>-v6-w20` / `-w40`.

```
Fast, dark British folk talking blues: one acoustic guitar strummed hard and fast, bone dry, nothing else. Two English men rattle off the story at speed in a flat, fast-talking voice, raw and furious, spitting every line, voices cracking with rage: a weathered working-class bloke and a plummy posh gent. Bleak, vicious and relentless.
```

Once again and you catching my eye
and you looking to the side in shame but why
now let me explain how I'm just poor
you keep on walking through that Wait trose door
presenting yourself with your shiny teeth
fucking sense of entitlement and self belief
I get that you think your deals are slick
but I bet that you paid for your wheels on tick!
cash from the bank for your wank tank
four tonnes of steel just to get a meal deal
you got cheese but I want Cheddar
I can't live like this forever
I might be insane but I do want change
let's see what we can arrange!
now I insist that I hold that door
please sir can I FUCKIN have some more?

you are intent on living in a tent
it's a lack of work ethic it's pathetic
getting parra lettic it seems that you are just a bum
drowning your sorrow until tomorrow comes

What about if we taxed the rich?
what the fuck you think this is BITCH!
I work hard to pay for my yard
Payin my tax with a platinum card

the only thing I'm changing is the lane in my M3

now please let me drink my shatoe nerf doo pap

[weathered bloke]
Oh shit here we both are living in a car
park rained on in the fucking dark
[plummy gent]

then I drank broke my back now I'm in the last part
[both men together]
the AI does the fast part now the real question is
will it allow because it's in charge now

in having the manager or any of the c-suite about
[weathered bloke]

and sicker than when the government debased us
[plummy gent]
back to that time when we very first met
I do regret that I judged you I was wrong
[weathered bloke]
yet I don't begrudge you
it's us and them now
[plummy gent]
well we don't have long
and by the time it hits we'll be gone
[end]

### r46 tbfast-industrial atom

Style:

```
Industrial folk talking blues: a distorted acoustic guitar grinding one dark riff, a pounding industrial kick drum and clanking metal hits, harsh and mechanical. Two English men bark the story fast and furious, aggressive and relentless, every word hammered: a weathered working-class bloke and a plummy posh gent. Brutal, cold and violent.
```

## v6.37 Round r45 — the liked talking blues, done like *Kate McCannon* (2026-09-16)

**Brief, Jack 2026-09-16:** *"Please do something like the one i like before but do it like Colter Wall - Kate McCannon."* Base: `r42 talkingblues atom` (§v6.34, Jack's pick). Same lyrics, same two English men, same settings. Only the Style and Exclude boxes move.

**The reference, read and not heard** (no artist name goes in a box):
- A **murder ballad** (2017), told by the killer. It takes its time, and the murder lands in the last lines ([Songtell](https://songtell.com/colter-wall/kate-mccannon), [College of Charleston blog](https://blogs.charleston.edu/engl-370-02/2023/04/24/gothicism-in-kate-mccannon/)).
- Music: "somber guitar chords and tight drumming". **A minor** (G · Am · C · Dm), guitar tuned down a whole step ([Charleston](https://blogs.charleston.edu/engl-370-02/2023/04/24/gothicism-in-kate-mccannon/), [Chordify](https://chordify.net/chords/colter-wall-songs/kate-mccannon-chords), [Ultimate Guitar](https://tabs.ultimate-guitar.com/tab/colter-wall/kate-mccannon-chords-1997499)). ⬜ The tempo sources disagree (SongBPM says 143, a tab says 60), so no BPM is set; `slow` is used.
- The album is **sparse**, "emphasizing his storytelling with a nearly cinematic quality": his "resonant and raw baritone", the "deep rumble of his world-weary baritone", fingerstyle acoustic, "steady kick-drum stomping". Dave Cobb produced it, with drums, **pedal steel** and piano ([Broadway World](https://www.broadwayworld.com/bwwmusic/article/Colter-Walls-Debut-Album-Out-Today-20170512), [Wikipedia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Colter_Wall), [Studio 1 Vintage](https://studio1-vintage.com/en-us/blogs/in-the-groove/in-the-groove-ep-4-colter-wall-s-t)).

| Lane | What changes from the liked atom | Unbanned |
|---|---|---|
| `mcc-talk` | murder ballad · fingerpicked minor-key guitar · slow soft kick stomp · faint pedal steel · a **deep world-weary** near-speaking voice, low gravelly rumble | `drums`, `country` |
| `mcc-sung` | the reference is **sung**, so this lane lets them sing low and plain: deep resonant baritones, "more telling than singing" | + `singing`, `sung vocals`, `vocal melody`, `melodic` |
| `mcc-stomp` | the "tight drumming" pushed forward: steady stomping kick + tight snare + pedal steel, still near-speaking | `drums`, `country` |

🔴 **Accent risk is high.** The reference is a Western-drawl country singer, and `pedal steel`, `murder ballad` and `country` all sit in the American pool. `twang`, `americana`, `Southern drawl`, `bluegrass`, `banjo` and `harmonica` stay banned, and the voice sentence still says `English` twice. If they come back American, delete `pedal steel` first.

Settings = the liked take's: v6 · SI 80 · Variety Off · Max Mode ON · Male · Personalize off · no Voice · Auto · camping-Jack · Weirdness **20** and **30**. Titles `camping-r45-mcc-<lane>-v6-w20` / `-w30`.

**Generated 2026-09-16 (Jack's ask):** 6 Creates made 12 takes at 20 credits each (3,730 → 3,610). Every load passed its checks.
- **mcc-talk** · w20: [5ad2e811](https://suno.com/song/5ad2e811-66ab-4c6b-9e2e-1f72e17f3063) 3:30 · w20: [7869b44e](https://suno.com/song/7869b44e-0c5b-46b0-8af6-6fe8c40b48e3) 3:30 · w30: [cffe3973](https://suno.com/song/cffe3973-765d-4e73-bebf-c3b989742cf5) 3:27 · w30: [d444167b](https://suno.com/song/d444167b-a95f-48fe-b0cf-f281b4265697) 3:33

> ⭐ **Jack's pick, 2026-09-16:** *"camping-r42-talkingblues-v6-w20 i like this one, please do varied versions of it."* ⬜ Which of the two w20 takes ([51d749bc](https://suno.com/song/51d749bc-dead-4550-8885-e6015d0024df) / [4431429f](https://suno.com/song/4431429f-7402-4792-ac7a-39a4711e68ce)) isn't recorded. The atom is §v6.34 `r42 talkingblues atom`.

Two kinds of variation, kept apart so each teaches something:

**A · `tb-same`: the exact atom, not one word changed** (a slider round). Variety grid **Normal · High · Extra** at weirdness 20. Variety is Suno's own control for rewriting the Style box, so this gives "the same song, reimagined by Suno" (undetectr, §v6.33). If one lands, its rewritten Style is on the take's Suno record and can be read back, like d13ca10a in §v6.0.

**B · four one-change twists.** The voice sentence, lyrics, excludes and settings are the liked take's. Only the music sentence changes:
| Twist | Music sentence | Unbanned |
|---|---|---|
| `tb-band` | + a walking double bass and a soft brushed snare | `drums` |
| `tb-dark` | minor key, one slide acoustic guitar moaning a slow figure | — |
| `tb-fast` | one acoustic guitar strumming a quick driving train rhythm | — |
| `tb-noir` | one moody tremolo electric guitar, slow repeating riff | — |

Settings = the liked take's: v6 · SI 80 · Variety **Off** (B) · Max Mode ON · Male · Personalize off · no Voice · Auto · camping-Jack. B runs at weirdness **20** (the winner) and **30**. Titles `camping-r44-tb-<twist>-v6-w20` / `-w30`, and `camping-r44-tb-same-v6-var-<step>-w20`.

### r44 tb-band atom

Style:

```
British folk talking blues with a small band: an acoustic guitar picking a simple repeating three-chord line, a walking double bass and a soft brushed snare, all kept low in the background. Voice-forward mix, voices loud, dry and close: two English men telling the story in a flat, rhythmic, near-speaking voice, dry and wry, a weathered working-class bloke and a plummy posh gent. Bleak, deadpan and droll.
```

Exclude styles:

```
singing, sung vocals, sung chorus, vocal melody, melodic, harmonies, choir, crooning, falsetto, autotune, female vocal, hook, anthemic, pop, spoken word, performance poetry, poetry slam, recital, recitation, declamatory, dramatic reading, country, twang, americana, Southern drawl, bluegrass, banjo, harmonica, full band, comedic, novelty, parody
```

Lyrics:

```lyrics
[Verse 1 | weathered bloke]
Once again, and you catching my eye,
and you looking to the side in shame, but why
now, let me explain, how I'm just poor
you keep on walking, through that Wait trose door
presenting yourself, with your shiny teeth
fucking sense of entitlement, and self belief
I get, that you think your deals are slick
but I bet, that you paid for your wheels on tick!
cash from the bank for your wank tank
four tonnes of steel, just to get a meal deal
you got cheese but I want Cheddar
I can't live like this forever
I might be insane but I do want change,
let's see what we can arrange!
now, I insist that I hold that door
please sir, can I FUCKIN, have some more?
[Verse 2 | plummy gent]
you are intent on living in a tent
it's a lack of work ethic, it's pathetic,
getting parra lettic, it seems that you are just a bum
drowning your sorrow until tomorrow comes
prospects exist and now I insist
that you just stop the grift
What about if we taxed the rich?
what the fuck you think this is, BITCH!
I work hard to pay for my yard
Payin my tax with a platinum card
you want change but my pockets are empty
the only thing I'm changing, is the lane in my M3
if you worked hard, then you could have plenty, fenty,
all you now seem to do, is resent me.
wealth gap? fuckin what a load of crap
now please let me drink my shatoe nerf doo pap
[Last verse | the two men trade lines]
[weathered bloke]
Oh shit, here we both are, living in a car
park, rained on in the fucking dark
[plummy gent]
went down the wrong track, then I got the sack,
then I drank, broke my back, now I'm in the last part
[both men together]
the AI does the fast part, now, the real question is
will it allow, because it's in charge now...
[plummy gent]
you see as it turns out, there is very little clout,
in having the manager or any of the c-suite about
[weathered bloke]
the speed the robots replaced us was quicker
and sicker than when the government debased us
[plummy gent]
back to that time when we very first met,
I do regret that I judged you, I was wrong,
[weathered bloke]
yet I don't begrudge you,
it's us and them now
[plummy gent]
well we don't have long
and by the time it hits, we'll be gone
[end]
```

## v6.35 Round r43 — fast, aggressive, emotional: grime MCs over a Hans Zimmer-style film score (2026-09-16)

**Brief, Jack 2026-09-16:** *"I did not like any of them [r42], please find other ones that match the fast paced aggressive and emotional, with a powerful instrumental hans zimmer vibe that doesnt have to be that genre behind it mix. maybe with lofi thrown in there. english grime rapper for the vocals."*

So r41/r42's "music quiet in the background" rule is **lifted**. The instrumental is powerful again, with the voices still in front.

🔑 **From our own history, the most useful fact:** the "Hans Zimmer layer" Jack liked in **r10 `ukg`** came from `cheap synth strings, night-bus melancholy`. That box never named an orchestra (§v6.5). When r14–r16 swapped in a *real* orchestra, Jack said (r17 brief) it "did not sound like Hans Zimmer". That fits the research: Zimmer's sound is **hybrid**, synths and processed sound design blended with the orchestra, a driving **ostinato** (a short repeating figure), **braams**, and a low pulsing engine ([ROLI](https://roli.com/blog/happy-birthday-hans-zimmer-a-deep-dive), [Professional Composers](https://professionalcomposers.com/how-to-make-hybrid-orchestral-music-like-hans-zimmer/), [Filipe Leitão](https://www.filipeleitao.com/post/how-to-write-epic-string-ostinatos-like-hans-zimmer)). **So `cheap synth strings` is in every lane**, alongside ostinato and braam words.

**Five lanes, all found by research:**
| Lane | What it is | Source |
|---|---|---|
| `protest` | British protest rap over a harsh orchestral string-stab loop. Plan B's *Ill Manors* sampled Shostakovich's 7th and roughed it up to sound "harsher… rougher and tougher" | [Wikipedia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ill_Manors_(song)), [WhoSampled](https://www.whosampled.com/sample/142229/Plan-B-Ill-Manors-Peter-Fox-Alles-Neu/), [Arcana](https://arcana.fm/2015/02/15/the-borrowers-plan-b-ill-manors/) |
| `hybrid` | cinematic grime: a hybrid film score fused with a grime beat (ostinato, braams, ticking pulse, war drums) | [cinematic rap beats](https://tellingbeatzz.com/cinematic-type-beats/), [Suno cinematic prompts](https://hookgenius.app/learn/suno-epic-cinematic-prompts/). Pair "epic" with a real genre, never alone ([Jack Righteous](https://jackrighteous.com/en-us/blogs/guides-using-suno-ai-music-creation/suno-ai-epic-modifier-guide)) |
| `lofi` | lo-fi cinematic grime: tape hiss, wobble and crunch over a sad piano plus a film-score swell | tape saturation on "orchestral stacks, gritty drum breaks" ([Lofi Weekly](https://lofiweekly.com/2026/03/02/unlocking-unique-vintage-atmosphere-using-tape-emulation-plugins-for-hip-hop-and-lofi-beats/), [LANDR](https://blog.landr.com/lofi/)). `dusty`/`vinyl` avoided (US crate words, accent risk) |
| `dnbscore` | emotional grime over 174 BPM D&B with the score, the direction r10/r11 came from | our own r10 → r11 log |
| `drillscore` | orchestral UK drill: violin/cello riffs plus braams, sliding bass | violin loops "ideal for UK Drill, Grime" ([Looperman](https://www.looperman.com/loops/tags/free-violin-loops-samples-sounds-wavs-download)) |

**Vocals, every lane:** the same cast sentence. Two English grime MCs, *"one gruff and raw, one clipped and cold, both spitting fast, furious and emotional, voices close to cracking"*. Singing, choir and `operatic vocals` are banned (epic scores pull choirs in).
**Words:** the canon §4 48 lines, checked by script. The cues add an instrumental intro and an **instrumental break before the bridge**, so the score gets a moment to hit. No new words.
**Bold check:** outside the shared cast sentence, the most any two Style boxes share is 28 chars (`into a huge film-score rise`).

Settings, all five: v6 · SI 80 · Variety Off · Max Mode ON · Male · Personalize off · no Voice · Auto · camping-Jack · Weirdness 20 and 40. Titles `camping-r43-<lane>-v6-w20` / `-w40`.

**Generated 2026-09-16 (Jack's ask):** 10 Creates made 20 takes at 20 credits each (4,150 → 3,950). Every load passed its checks. Takes run ~3:00–3:50.
- **protest** · w20: [91a7d0a6](https://suno.com/song/91a7d0a6-8796-48b3-9d48-030dce3c0db2) · w20: [f413c0cd](https://suno.com/song/f413c0cd-b3f6-4e68-8b34-fd25eb1a715c) · w40: [13954c85](https://suno.com/song/13954c85-b011-4d14-a953-1506839626dc) · w40: [9a057c71](https://suno.com/song/9a057c71-8f01-4642-8b1a-2e08c3c71107)

### r43 hybrid atom

Style:

```
Cinematic grime at 140 BPM, a hybrid film score fused with a grime beat, one piece of music: a fast urgent string ostinato and cheap synth strings, huge low braams, a ticking clock pulse and big war drums over hard grime drums and a growling sub bass. ## v6.34 Round r42 — five storytelling traditions (2026-09-16)

**Brief, Jack 2026-09-16:** *"Basically i want to tell the camping story via song, please research the internet for the best way to do that in what style/genre/vibe"*, then *"Please try all of them."*

**The research (in chat, 2026-09-16), ranked for Camping:**
1. **The Streets-style storytelling rap:** a British "rap opera" about ordinary life, "aggressively conversational" over "sparse beats" ([Wikipedia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/A_Grand_Don't_Come_for_Free), [DIY](https://diymag.com/feature/looking-back-on-the-streets-a-grand-dont-come-for-free)). Close to r10 `ukg`, which Jack asked to get back to.
2. **Sleaford Mods-style talk-rap:** "wry, heavily accented talk-sung rants" about class over minimal loops ([Rolling Stone](https://www.rollingstone.com/music/music-features/sleaford-mods-meet-englands-finest-deadpan-blue-collar-talk-rap-duo-127039/), [Wikipedia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sleaford_Mods)).
3. **Rap battle (Hamilton's Cabinet Battles):** policy argued as a two-man rap clash ([Wikipedia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cabinet_Battle)). Camping's verse 1 / verse 2 / bridge already has that shape.
4. **Talking blues** (Woody Guthrie): "a rhythmic, flat tone very near to a speaking voice", rhyming couplets, a repeating guitar line, political satire ([Wikipedia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Talking_blues)).
5. **Kae Tempest-style story:** one performer voicing several characters over synths and bass ([Wikipedia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Let_Them_Eat_Chaos)).

Also: repeated short phrases are what listeners recall ([PMC](https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/pmc/articles/PMC10585939/)). A refrain was proposed but **not added**, because repeating a line changes the canon words. ⬜ Jack/Kai to rule.

**Kept from r41** (the from-scratch rules): canon §4 words only, checked by script · music quiet in the background · voice-forward · no singing · short Style box (54–63 words) · Variety Off · Style Influence 80 · weirdness 20/40. No artist is named in any box. Each tradition is described by its sound. Talking-blues bans the American folk family, and the poetry-reading pool is banned wherever the delivery is talky. The bracket labels follow each tradition (`Round 1/2` for the battle, `Monologue` for the Tempest lane). Largest shared Style run: 104 chars, all of it the cast plus the brief's constant.

🔴 **Risks:** talking blues goes American (the genre pool) · the Sleaford-style lane may drift into poetry reading · UK garage may bring in an R&B singer (singing banned).

Settings, all five: v6 · SI 80 · Variety Off · Max Mode ON · Male · Personalize off · no Voice · Auto · camping-Jack · Weirdness 20 and 40. Titles `camping-r42-<lane>-v6-w20` / `-w40`.

**Generated 2026-09-16 (Jack's ask):** 10 Creates made 20 takes at 20 credits each (4,350 → 4,150). Every load passed its checks. Takes run ~2:15–3:10.
- **streets** · w20: [b188e107](https://suno.com/song/b188e107-4e3f-40eb-bd98-3ff799f943ac) · w20: [8b43ec3d](https://suno.com/song/8b43ec3d-b559-4aee-817e-256706df6dc4) · w40: [4b9346de](https://suno.com/song/4b9346de-9b31-420e-8063-9eb7fb1652b0) · w40: [7065d3ae](https://suno.com/song/7065d3ae-c30d-4f73-998b-590abe2d0aea)

## v6.33 Round r41 — FROM SCRATCH: background music, the canon words, no singing (2026-09-16)

**Brief, Jack 2026-09-16:** *"You are not making the instrumental parts simple, they should be very in the background, basically background music, please make sure you look up the lyrics in the repo and do those, forget the prompts before this we are starting from scratch, since everything else is not working. The opera ones are singing so fix that. use suno skills and research the internet for other people getting stuck on suno, with flat/dull/bad songs."*

🔑 **Every earlier round is ignored on purpose.** Nothing in r41 is inherited from r1–r40 except the house rules and the two English voice castings.

**The words: the canon, not r38's.** The 48 lines are §4 Lyrics below (Kai's round-17 final cut, the words canon per `songs/README.md`), checked line for line by script. r38–r40 had carried an invented `Please sir…` chorus, a new Verse 4, and `Ooh` intros that were never approved. They're gone. Structure follows §4's own casting: Verse 1 the gravelly man, Verse 2 the posh man, Bridge traded in §4's order. There are **no instrumental drops**, because the music is only background now.

**What the research says makes Suno flat, and what r41 does about each:**

| Finding (from other people's Suno trouble) | r41 |
|---|---|
| An overloaded Style box averages into generic output. More than ~7–10 descriptors conflict. The typical trap is writing a longer prompt after every bad take ([hookgenius](https://hookgenius.app/learn/better-music-from-suno/), [roo](https://roo.beehiiv.com/p/suno-ai-prompt-guide-2026-copy-paste-templates-the-formula-that-actually-works), [Jack Righteous: too complicated](https://jackrighteous.com/en-us/blogs/guides-using-suno-ai-music-creation/suno-prompt-too-complicated-clean-workflow), [Jack Righteous: same bad song](https://jackrighteous.com/en-us/blogs/guides-using-suno-ai-music-creation/how-to-stop-regenerating-the-same-bad-suno-song)) | Style boxes cut from **650–750 chars to 310–333** (~50 words): one genre, one sentence of background music, one of the voices |
| Put the voice first and say `voice-forward mix`. For talking, use a *minimal underscore* and remove `melodic`/`anthemic`/`hook`/`chorus` ([Jack Righteous: speak instead of sing](https://jackrighteous.com/en-us/blogs/guides-using-suno-ai-music-creation/suno-ai-spoken-narration-workflow), [hookgenius vocal prompts](https://hookgenius.app/learn/suno-vocal-prompts/)) | `storytelling` in genre position · `kept far in the background` · `Voice-forward mix, voices loud, dry and close`. `hook, anthemic, pop, melodic` banned. The lyrics have **no chorus** |
| v6 Variety rewrites your prompt, so set it to 0. v6 "wants to be directed exactly": Style Influence **75–85**, weirdness low (~20) ([undetectr](https://undetectr.com/blog/suno-v6-changing-your-prompt)) | Variety **Off** · Style Influence **80** · weirdness pair **20 / 40** |
| Songs sound muffled or dull partly because the raw audio is unmastered. That's fixed after generation with EQ on the stems, not in the prompt ([Jack Righteous etc. via search](https://orustech.substack.com/p/suno-ai-pro-level-songs)) | noted. If the takes are right but dull, the next step is stems plus a mix, not another prompt |
| Contradictory directions average out (e.g. `raw but polished`) | one mood per lane: `Dark and serious` |

**Why the opera takes sang (r39, r40):** r40's Style box literally asked for `sing the hook plainly`, and its lyrics had a chorus. Remove the chorus and the singing instruction, add `no singing` to Style (the house talking recipe allows this one negation), and ban the whole singing family in Exclude. The opera lives only in the **background music** (`operatic orchestral underscore`, `faint tragic opera strings`).

Shared between lanes (198 chars, opera/operadnb): the cast plus the brief's constant (background music, voice-forward, no singing). The music clause is each lane's own.

Settings, all three: v6 · Style Influence **80** · Variety Off · Max Mode ON · Male · Personalize off · no Voice · Duration Auto · camping-Jack · Weirdness **20 and 40**. Titles `camping-r41-<lane>-v6-w20` / `-w40`.

**Generated 2026-09-16 (Jack's ask):** 6 Creates made 12 takes at 20 credits each (4,470 → 4,350). Every load passed its checks first time. Takes run **~2:30–2:45**, down from r39's 5 minutes, because there's no chorus and no drops.

## v6.32 Round r40 — `opera` · `operadnb`, with normal voices (2026-09-16)

**Correction, Jack 2026-09-16:** *"when i said opera, it should be a normal voice, not an opera singer, please do these again."* So r39 misread the brief: **"opera" means the MUSIC is opera, not the singers.** r40 redoes both opera lanes; r39's eskibeat stands.

- **The music:** an operatic orchestra (the pit orchestra of a tragic Italian opera: strings, timpani, woodwinds/brass). The opera + D&B lane lays it over a 174 BPM break with a unity sentence.
- **The voices:** two ordinary English men in their own normal voices. They talk and rap the story, and sing the chorus plainly in untrained voices. The casting is r37's (`a low, gruff Bristol voice` · `a clipped BBC newsreader voice`), the favourite round's.
- **The lever** (suno-tag-mechanics.md, the fusion section): operatic words invite opera *singing*, so the Exclude box bans the whole opera-voice family: `opera singer, operatic vocals, classical singing, bel canto, soprano, tenor, baritone, bass-baritone, vibrato, aria, sustained vocal notes, angelic voices, choir, choral, falsetto, musical theatre, Broadway, operetta`. The Style box never says `operatic vocals` or `singer`.
- The two Style boxes share 74 characters (11%), and all of it is the cast clause, which is allowed.

🔴 **Risk:** `Italian opera` may still pull a singer in on the chorus. If it does, delete `Italian opera` next round rather than adding more bans.

Settings: as r39 (v6 · SI 75 · Variety Off · Max Mode ON · Male · Personalize off · no Voice · Auto · camping-Jack · Weirdness 30 and 40). Titles `camping-r40-<lane>-v6-w30` / `-w40`.

**Generated 2026-09-16 (Jack's ask):** 4 Creates made 8 takes at 20 credits each (4,550 → 4,470). Nothing was spent on the failed attempts. The first opera pair was stopped twice before Create (`model:NO-MENU`). Suno's model menu now mounts slower than the loader's fixed 800ms wait, so the check read no menu and left it open over the lyrics box. Fixed in `suno.mts` `openModelMenu` (it now waits for the menu items, up to 5s), after which it went through.
- **opera** · w30: [1daad796](https://suno.com/song/1daad796-6c0b-457a-9499-c1ad90dd5830) · [15b82e55](https://suno.com/song/15b82e55-9f7d-4a96-8a8d-020be7693166) · w40: [95d8f422](https://suno.com/song/95d8f422-6dae-440e-bcac-ea43499a9339) · [f9531b2d](https://suno.com/song/f9531b2d-685f-4fff-af16-56adf3d58a9d)

### r40 opera atom

Style:

```
A dark, tragic operatic orchestral score in D minor, like the pit orchestra of a grand Italian opera: sweeping strings, a grand piano, rolling timpani and mournful woodwinds, rising and falling with the story, no drums and no beat. Over it, two ordinary English men tell the story in their own normal voices, half talking and half rapping, close, dry and loud in front of the orchestra: a low, gruff Bristol voice, bitter and weary, and a clipped BBC newsreader voice, cold and cutting. On the chorus the orchestra swells while both men sing the hook plainly in untrained natural voices. Tragic and serious, played completely straight.
```

### r40 operadnb atom

Style:

```
Drum and bass at 174 BPM fused with a dark operatic orchestra, one piece of music, not a remix of one by the other: a rolling chopped breakbeat and a deep sub bass under sweeping tragic strings, pounding timpani and a mournful brass line, like a grand Italian opera overture played over the break. Two ordinary English men rap the story fast and tight on the beat in their own normal voices, dry and loud in front: a low, gruff Bristol MC, bitter and menacing, and a clipped BBC newsreader voice, cold and cutting. On the chorus the drums drop out under the strings while both men chant the hook plainly together, then the break slams back in. Dark, grand and relentless.
```

## v6.31 Round r39 — back to square one: `opera` · `operadnb` · `eskibeat` (2026-09-16)

> 🔴 **The opera and operadnb lanes below misread "opera"** (opera singers instead of opera music). Jack wanted normal voices, so they were redone in r40 (§v6.32). The eskibeat lane stands.

**Brief, Jack 2026-09-16:** *"start from square one… it is a story. so maybe that is the best way, simple music with rap/spoken word/talking/singing over the music. can we try a few versions, like opera, then opera with drum and bass, then grime with eskibeat."*

Three **bold** lanes (automation skill: rewrite, don't patch). The words are r38's, byte for byte (only the bracket cues change, checked by script). The longest run of text any two Style boxes share is 22 characters (under 4%). Each lane's excludes ban the other lanes' sounds. All three keep the music **simple** so the story carries.

**Research (read, not heard):**
- **Opera, and why recitative.** Opera already has a form for telling the plot fast: **recitative**, sung speech on repeated notes that follows the rhythm of the words, with sparse accompaniment. Songs (arias) are where it stops to feel something. So: verses = recitative, chorus = aria. Plain operatic words (`operatic`, `bass-baritone`, `tenor`) are the terms that work in Suno. Naming a vocal range stops the melody jumping between octaves ([Suno vocal prompts](https://sunoaipromptguide.org/suno-vocal-prompts/), [hookgenius](https://hookgenius.app/learn/suno-vocal-effects/), [liadigi](https://www.liadigi.com/2025/09/opera-robotic-javanese-vocals-on-suno.html)). ⚠️ `English chamber opera` is a British tradition, but `operetta`/`patter song` pull comic Gilbert & Sullivan (the satire trap), so they're banned. Our own caution (suno-tag-mechanics.md): `operatic` invites opera *singing*. Here that's the point, so `operatic vocals`/`vibrato` are **not** banned.
- **Opera + D&B.** It's a niche pairing (High Contrast is the name DnB forums credit with doing it well) ([DOA forum](https://www.dogsonacid.com/threads/what-orchestral-drum-and-bass-tracks-have-you-heard.785725/)). Lead with D&B + a unity sentence ("one piece of music, not a remix"). Stripped to break, sub and one bell so the voices have room. The chorus is a drums-out event, not a bar count.
- **Eskibeat.** Wiley's early-2000s "cold, spacey" grime: **140 BPM** (FL Studio's default tempo), *"gliding squarewave basslines, the hollow, digital rumbles"*, icy metallic stabs, *"the Eski click — a ping-ponging sound effect straight out of an 8-bit Nintendo game"*, *"drums that sound like slamming car doors"*, sometimes landing *"on the wrong beat"*, *"sparse and shocking"*, *"cold, mechanical, strange"* ([Complex: A Journey Through Wiley's Eskibeat](https://www.complex.com/music/a/son-raw/a-journey-through-wiley-eskibeat), [Wikipedia: Grime](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Grime_music), [Wikipedia: Eskimo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eskimo_(grime_beat))). Described without naming Wiley.

🔴 **Casting risks, written down before listening:**
- **Grime casts young London MCs** (suno-voices.md §2b). Right for the lane, but it won't sound like a fifty-something Bob, and a *posh* MC may not come.
- **Recitative may come back fully sung** (no talking at all), or as musical theatre. Broadway and musical theatre are banned.
- **Opera + D&B:** the operatic voice may lose to the genre's default liquid-DnB singer.

Settings, all three lanes: v6 · Style Influence 75 · Variety Off · Max Mode ON · Male · Personalize off · no Voice · Duration Auto · camping-Jack · Weirdness 30 and 40. Titles `camping-r39-<lane>-v6-w30` / `-w40`.

**Generated 2026-09-16 (Jack's ask):** 6 Creates made 12 takes at 20 credits each with Max Mode (4,670 → 4,550). 🔴 **Balance was 4,670 at the start, not r38's 4,690.** 20 credits went somewhere between rounds (another session, or a hand generation). The eskibeat w40 Create was stopped by the model check (the model read back null) before spending anything. The form read v6 on recheck, and a w40-only rerun went through.
- **opera** · w30: [13a922ec](https://suno.com/song/13a922ec-ac7c-48c4-8628-fda246f23548) 4:58 · [0a3d6b9d](https://suno.com/song/0a3d6b9d-d22c-4839-88c9-86b7f7d44642) 5:07 · w40: [27761ccc](https://suno.com/song/27761ccc-f4df-4dc0-a741-73402179a292) 5:12 · [d655d187](https://suno.com/song/d655d187-7c84-4897-83ae-81572a21ffce) 4:58

### r39 opera atom

Style:

```
English chamber opera in D minor, tragic, bare and intimate, played completely straight. The whole accompaniment is one grand piano playing slow, soft held chords, with a low cello drone underneath, and nothing else, so the words carry the story. The verses are recitative: sung speech that tells the story quickly on repeated notes, following the natural rhythm of the English words. Two male opera singers take turns, a rough, dark bass-baritone, bitter and weathered, and a bright, haughty English tenor, crisp and cold. The chorus opens into an aria, both men singing in full operatic voice with rich vibrato, rising to one long held high note, aching and defiant. Perfect English diction, the voices close and in front, the piano far behind them.
```

### r39 eskibeat atom

Style:

```
Early 2000s East London grime at 140 BPM, eskibeat: cold, icy, sparse and mechanical, made on a bedroom computer for pirate radio. A gliding square-wave bassline, hollow and digital, icy metallic synth stabs, a pinging 8-bit click bouncing left and right, and crashing kick drums like slamming car doors, in an off-kilter, broken rhythm full of gaps. Lo-fi, raw, mono and loud, nothing else in the beat. Two London MCs spit hard, fast and furious on the beat, trading verses: a low, gruff, angry MC and a sharp, sneering posh MC. The chorus is a chanted hook shouted by both together. Raw, cold and confrontational, one hard loop from start to finish.
```

Exclude styles:

```
UK drill, drill, trap, 808, sliding 808, boom bap, American rap, US hip hop, drum and bass, dubstep, house, UK garage, 2-step, afroswing, dancehall, Jamaican accent, patois, ragga, R&B, singing, sung chorus, melodic rap, autotune, piano, strings, guitar, orchestral, opera, jazz, lo-fi hip hop, laid-back, slow flow, mumbled, female vocal, pop, comedic, novelty, parody, happy, major key, tempo change
```

[low gruff MC, talking over the intro, hyping it up]
Yeah this one's about camping not the fun kind no marshmallows
the kind where the tent's your address
and the bloke in the big black motor thinks you picked it
Right let it drop
[Verse 1 | the drums crash in | low gruff MC, spitting fast and hard on the beat, furious]
Once again and you catching my eye
and you looking to the side in shame but why
now let me explain how I'm just poor

## v6.30 Round r38 — `bucktown-copy` (2026-09-16)

**Brief, Jack 2026-09-16:** *"do a complete copy of bucktown, the beat the style everything, but keep the english rapper voice, the kaleo chorus."*

**What "complete copy" can and can't mean here.** Suno refuses artist and song names, and a take that imitates a real artist can get the account flagged. The sample (Jack Bruce, *Born to Be Blue*) can't be reproduced, and the words stay ours. So this copies **everything describable**: tempo, key, era, drum sound, bass, sample character, sparseness, the verse-trading form. That was researched in r37 (§v6.29): 89 BPM, G♯/A♭, Da Beatminerz, *"dark and gritty… heavy filtered basslines, crisp snares, atmospheric jazz samples… raw and murky… bottomless"*, SP-1200 grit.

| Bucktown | Into the Style box |
|---|---|
| 89 BPM, G♯/A♭ | `89 BPM in A-flat minor` (⬜ major/minor not in the source; minor matches the record's darkness) |
| early-90s SP-1200 / S950 grit | `a raw, gritty 1994 underground sampler beat… crunchy 12-bit sampler drums` (era anchor + the machine's sound, no model names) |
| thumping kick, crisp snare | `a hard thumping kick and a crisp cracking snare on a head-nodding swing` |
| filtered, bottomless bass | `a heavy filtered jazz bassline rolling deep underneath` |
| muted jazz sample loop | `a dark muffled jazz sample loop with a moody piano and a muted horn, looping and bottomless`. ⬜ The instruments in the loop are a guess, not heard |
| sparse, menacing, no extra layers | `sparse and menacing, nothing else in the way`. **All r30a trip hop layers removed** (piano loop, strings, melodica); `trip hop, strings, orchestral, melodica, synthesizer` banned |
| two MCs trading verses | `two English rappers trading verses` |

**Kept as asked:**
- **English rapper voices** from r37 (`a Bristol MC… low and menacing, rugged and aggressive, quick and tight` · `a clipped BBC newsreader voice, cold and cutting`), plus UK hip hop in genre position.
- **The Kaleo chorus** from r31, **only on the chorus this time**. In r31 the raspy texture sat on the verse voice too, and that's what Jack called "crazy". The verses keep r37's rap voice. Cues: `raw raspy bluesy singer, belting and aching` ×3, `wordless` intro/outro. `shouted` and `falsetto` are unbanned so the belt can crack up high.

🔴 **Accent risk is at its highest here.** The beat recipe *is* the American boom-bap pool. `boom bap`, `East Coast`, `Brooklyn`, `dusty`, `vinyl` and `scratches` are **left out on purpose** (suno-voices.md §4c), and there are no American-family bans (§4c: a ban that has failed three times gets deleted). Blues belts drift American too. If the voices come back American, the next step is Thread 4's cut: generate the verses and the chorus separately.
Settings: v6 · **Style Influence 75** (a close copy wants adherence) · Variety Off · Max Mode ON · Male · Personalize off · no Voice · Auto · camping-Jack · Weirdness 30 and 40. Titles `camping-r38-bucktowncopy-v6-w30` / `-w40`.

**Generated 2026-09-16 (Jack's standing ask)**: 2 Creates made 4 takes at 20 credits each with Max Mode (4,730 → 4,690). No unexplained credit moves this round.

w40: [30331cb8](https://suno.com/song/30331cb8-e23a-48b4-ac56-1b0dbff3102f) · [a8a4225f](https://suno.com/song/a8a4225f-b26b-4186-b7a8-c5ac517f7c0c) — ⬜ Jack hasn't listened yet. (The take list showed the w40 title with Style text stuck on the end, the same scraping glitch as r29.)

## v6.29 Round r37 — `bucktown` (2026-09-16)

> ⭐ **Jack's pick, 2026-09-16:** *"camping-r37-bucktown-v6-w30 love this one please remember this."* ⬜ Which of the two w30 takes (9cd4a7ec / 3535c148) he meant isn't recorded. Build later rounds from this atom.

**Brief, Jack 2026-09-16:** *"bring it back to this style camping-r30a-triphop-v6-w30 make a faster more agressive version but not too
fast, maybe make it like bucktown smif n wessun."*

**The reference, read and not heard.** *Bucktown* (1994, lead single off *Dah Shinin'*), produced by **Da Beatminerz** (DJ Evil Dee, Mr. Walt). It samples Jack Bruce's *Born to Be Blue* and The Whatnauts. **89 BPM, G♯/A♭**. The Beatminerz sound is *"dark and gritty… heavy, filtered basslines, crisp snares, atmospheric jazz samples… raw and murky… hypnotic and menacing… deep bass, eerie jazz samples… seemingly bottomless"*, with the kick's thump prioritised and SP-1200 grit
([Wikipedia: Bucktown](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bucktown_(song)), [SongBPM](https://songbpm.com/@smif-n-wessun/bucktown),
[WhoSampled](https://www.whosampled.com/Smif-N-Wessun/Bucktown/), [Wikipedia: Da Beatminerz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Da_Beatminerz),
[Medium: Enta Da Stage](https://medium.com/@livefromfayettenam/black-moons-enta-da-stage-the-birth-of-boot-camp-clik-and-a-hardcore-classic-1b273bf7376e)).

🔑 **Bucktown is 89 BPM, the same speed as r30a's 90.** Its drive comes from the **drums and the delivery**, not the tempo. So "faster but not too fast" = **96 BPM** (a hardcore 1995 pocket is ~95 per [WaveCanvas](https://wavecanvas.io/suno-prompts/hip-hop)) plus hard, bouncing drums and a quicker rap.

| r30a | r37 | Why |
|---|---|---|
| `thudding, crackly vinyl breakbeat`, `record scratches` | `hard knocking gritty sampler drums, a thumping kick and a cracking snare with a relentless head-nodding bounce` | the Beatminerz drum, described **without** `boom bap`/`dusty`/`vinyl`/`scratches`, which are US crate-digging words that cast American voices (suno-voices.md §4c) |
| `deep rumbling sub bass` | `a heavy filtered jazz bassline walking underneath` + `a dark muffled jazz horn loop` + `murky and bottomless` | the Beatminerz low end and jazz sample. Piano loop, strings and melodica kept, so it's still r30a |
| `low, menacing, half-whispered rap` · `quiet, clipped, cold` | `low and menacing, a rugged aggressive rap, quick and tight on the beat, punching the end of every line` · `cold and cutting, just as quick` | aggression through **delivery words, not voice texture**. r31's texture words (`raw, raspy`, a belt) are what made it "crazy". `whispered, breathy, mumbled, lazy/laid-back, slow flow` banned; `shouted` stays banned |
| `never building, never dropping` | `one relentless loop start to finish` | same loop-based form, stated positively |
| (English, from r35) | `Bristol trip hop **and UK hip hop**` · `a Bristol MC` · `a clipped BBC newsreader voice` · `English singer`. The 8 American-family bans are deleted | 🔴 **Bucktown is a Brooklyn record, which is the American pool.** It is borrowed as beat mechanics only, never by name or scene. Voices stay r35's English casting |
| lyric labels `[mid-range rasp voice]` / `[crisp posh voice]` (mismatched, r34 finding) | `[low menacing voice]` / `[cold posh voice]`, `trade lines` | one casting per man |

Also unbanned: `fast tempo`, `bouncy`, `brass section` (the horn loop). Words unchanged (r28's, new verse ⬜ still waiting for approval).
Settings = r30a's: v6 · SI 70 · Variety **Off** · Max Mode ON · Male · Personalize off · no Voice · Auto · camping-Jack · Weirdness 30 and 40. Titles `camping-r37-bucktown-v6-w30` / `-w40`.

**Generated 2026-09-16 (Jack's standing ask)**: 2 Creates made 4 takes at 20 credits each with Max Mode (4,770 → 4,730). The form was left on v6-wild from r36; the loader switched it back to v6, and a read-back confirmed v6 before each Create.
w30: [9cd4a7ec](https://suno.com/song/9cd4a7ec-9c6f-47f6-b49d-47f660d8c08f) · [3535c148](https://suno.com/song/3535c148-bff6-4931-98f5-2dfc3f6a0570) ·
w40: [83b0f22d](https://suno.com/song/83b0f22d-efba-4340-91e3-81d6689ff22b) · [95993d97](https://suno.com/song/95993d97-6da5-443e-ae2e-563351264102) — ⬜ Jack hasn't listened yet.

## v6.28 Round r36 — `vary` (2026-09-16)

**Brief, Jack 2026-09-16, on r35:** *"all of those are way too similar please do ones that vary."*

**Why r35's four takes sounded alike:** every lever that makes takes differ was set to its tightest. **Variety Off** (*"Exact style"*, where both takes of a Create use one style), **Weirdness 30/40** (a gap of 10), plain v6 (the "reliable, precise" model), and a long, specific Style box at Style Influence 70.

**A slider round on r35's exact boxes. No prompt word changed.** Three spread axes:
- **Variety High / Max**: Suno *rewrites the Style box* per take (suno-v6.md §2, the vendor FAQ). 🔴 That means it can also rewrite away the English-accent fix and the gears. When one wins, copy **its stored Style** off the song page and rerun it at Variety Off.
- **Weirdness 30 vs 70/80**: a much wider gap than 30/40.
- **v6 Wild**: *"less predictable and more varied"*, used for hunting (suno-v6.md §1), with a noted quality drop and a tendency for the lyrics to drift.

Everything else = r35: SI 70 · Max Mode ON · Male · Personalize off · no Voice · Auto · camping-Jack.

## v6.27 Round r35 — `triphop-english` (2026-09-16)

**Brief, Jack 2026-09-16, on r34:** *"This is great build on it and make it better, Make the voice english though."*

### The accent audit on r34 (suno-voices.md §4c: count what names each nationality, in all boxes, weighted by position)

| | 🇺🇸 strong | 🇬🇧 strong | weak |
|---|---|---|---|
| Style | `crackly vinyl breakbeat` (crate-digging production slang), `record scratches` (US turntablism), `rap` | `Bristol` (genre position, but §2a says `trip hop` is a weak British lever) | `English`, `posh` (adjectives buy little) |
| Exclude | **11 names from the American family**: `American accent, American vocal, Southern drawl, US rap, trap, boom bap, jazz rap, lo-fi hip hop, americana, country vocal, gospel` | — | — |
| Lyrics | `trade bars` ×2 (`bars` is on §4c's US-family list) | — | — |

**It was an American prompt with British adjectives on it** (the §4c shape). §4c's rule, *"a ban that has failed three times is not free, delete it"*, applies to the Exclude list.

| Fix | Change |
|---|---|
| **Genre position** | `Bristol trip hop` → **`Bristol trip hop and UK hip hop`** (§2a: `UK hip hop` is a pool-selecting British word, the strongest lever there is) |
| **Cast a tradition, not an adjective** (§2b) | verse one → **`a Bristol MC`**: a place-name scene whose default performer is exactly this low, menacing, half-whispered delivery, plus `a broad English accent` as reinforcement. Verse two → **`a clipped BBC newsreader voice`** (the tested establishment-English tradition). Singer → `a haunting low English singer` |
| **Remove what names America** | `crackly vinyl` → `crunchy chopped`; `record scratches` → deleted; `vinyl crackle` → `tape hiss`; `trade bars` → `trade lines`. **The 11 American-family bans are deleted** from Exclude |

### Built on, and made better (the gears get two more steps)

- **A darker gear for verse four:** `a grinding distorted guitar drone joins in verse four`, the Mezzanine move, so the heaviest verse gets a new layer and not just more volume.
- **One breath of silence before the final chorus:** `everything cuts out for one breath before the final chorus lands hardest`, described as an event rather than a bar count (tag-mechanics "Making a fill or a one-off event audible"). It's mirrored in the `[Final Chorus]` cue.
- Everything else from r34 is kept: the layer jobs, the held-back strings and melodica, the voices conducted by section, the weird-voice bans, the subtle-orchestra bans. Words unchanged (new verse ⬜ still waiting for approval).

⬜ Still unproven: this is two jobs at once (accent + arrangement). If the accent is still wrong, the next step is Thread 4's cut, not more adjectives.
Settings = r34's. Titles `camping-r35-triphopenglish-v6-w30` / `-w40`.

### r35 triphop-english atom

Style:

```
Bristol trip hop and UK hip hop at 90 BPM in B-flat minor, dark, heavy and gripping, every layer in its own space: a deep round sub bass at the bottom, a crunchy chopped breakbeat punching in the middle, a haunting minor-key piano loop as the main motif, low sustained strings wide and far behind, and a melodica answering in the gaps between the vocal lines. Tape saturation, deep reverb on the strings, the voices dry, close and loud in front the whole way. It opens on the piano loop alone, the break and sub drop in with verse one, strings and melodica are held back for the first chorus, a grinding distorted guitar drone joins in verse four, and everything cuts out for one breath before the final chorus lands hardest. A haunting low English singer sings an aching chorus. Verse one: a Bristol MC, low, menacing and half-whispered, a broad English accent. Verse two: a posh Englishman, a clipped BBC newsreader voice, quiet and cold. Moody, paranoid and serious.
```

Exclude styles:

```
autotune, teenage voice, boyish voice, young MC, boy band, female lead vocal, sung verses, cheesy, comedic, novelty, parody, spoken word, tempo change, drum and bass, dubstep, EDM, big room, fast tempo, upbeat, happy, major key, bouncy, four on the floor, house, ska, brass section, synth-pop, rave, Jamaican accent, shouted, riser, epic orchestral, soaring strings, trailer music, film score, choir, falsetto, operatic, vibrato, robotic voice, vocoder, pitched-down voice, theatrical voice, cartoon voice, breathy ASMR, muffled vocals, buried vocals, mumbled, monotone, flat delivery, static arrangement, EDM riser
```

[cold posh voice]

back to that time when we very first met

yet I don't begrudge you it's us and them now
[cold posh voice]
well we don't have long and by the time it hits we'll be gone

Please sir can I have some more
still out here holding the door

ooh we don't have long

## v6.26 Round r34 — `triphop-deluxe` (2026-09-16)

**Brief, Jack 2026-09-16, on `camping-r30a-triphop-v6-w30`:** *"go heavy on this style improving it where you can making sure it
is not flat, the voice is not weird and the instrumentals are layered in a pleasing way."* Voice and words stay r30a's; the arrangement is rebuilt.

### What in r30a was working against each ask (read from its boxes)

| Ask | r30a had | Why it hurts | r34 |
|---|---|---|---|
| **not flat** | `one steady groove start to finish, never building, never dropping` + bans on `build, beat drop, crescendo` | literally asks for flat. It was added to stop trip hop ducking the voice, but it also removed every dynamic | **gears** (tag-mechanics "escalate the arrangement in gears"): piano alone → break + sub only → strings + melodica **held back until the first chorus** → verse 2 drops back down → verse 4 heavier → final chorus biggest. Written as absence → entrance → payoff in the Style box **and** mirrored in every section header. `build, beat drop, crescendo` unbanned; the voice guard `loud in front the whole way` and the `EDM riser` ban stay |
| **not flat** | every section cue at the same weight, three identical `[Chorus]` headers | *"many AI tracks feel flat all the way through" because every section sits at the same vocal weight* ([HookGenius](https://hookgenius.app/learn/suno-prompt-guide-2026/)) | the cues conduct: verse 1 half-whispered → verse 2 clipped and quiet → verse 4 `rising to a hard bitter edge` → `[Final Chorus]` a different tag, `the biggest moment` |
| **voice not weird** | 🔴 the Style box cast `low, menacing, half-whispered`, but the lyric labels (inherited from r28) said `[mid-range rasp voice]` / `[crisp posh voice]` | **two different castings for the same man.** Varied wording reads as a new character (suno-prompt "one fixed label per character") | labels now match the Style box: `[low menacing voice]` · `[cold posh voice]` · `[haunting low singer]`, repeated identically. Bans added on the weird-voice family: `falsetto, operatic, vibrato, robotic voice, vocoder, pitched-down voice, theatrical voice, cartoon voice, breathy ASMR, muffled/buried vocals, mumbled` |
| **layered pleasingly** | seven instruments listed in one breath with no jobs | named instruments all land in bar one (entrance rules), so everything plays at once and blurs together | **each layer gets a register and a job** (Mezzanine-style *space for every layer*, [Happy Mag](https://happymag.tv/engineering-the-sound-massive-attacks-mezzanine/)): sub at the bottom · break punching in the middle · piano loop = the motif · strings wide and far behind · melodica **in the gaps between vocal lines, never over the words** · scratches **only at section ends**. Production cues kept to 2–4 (tag-mechanics): tape saturation, reverb on the strings, dry close voices |

Also kept from r31/r32: the subtle-orchestra bans (`epic orchestral, soaring strings, film score, choir`) and the accent guard (`americana, country vocal, gospel`). Words = r28 = r30a, unchanged (new verse ⬜ still waiting for approval).
⬜ Nothing here is proven. It is several changes at once, so a win proves the whole package, not any single part of it.
Settings = r30a's: v6 · SI 70 · Variety Off · Max Mode ON · Male · Personalize off · no Voice · Auto · camping-Jack · Weirdness 30 and 40. Titles `camping-r34-triphopdeluxe-v6-w30` / `-w40`.

## v6.25 Round r33 — `dancin-triphop` (2026-09-16)

**Brief, Jack 2026-09-16:** *"make one where it combines this camping-r16-dancin-v6-w30 and camping-r30a-triphop-v6-w30."*
⬜ Both titles have two takes each, and which ones Jack means isn't recorded. This fuses the two **prompts** (§v6.8 r16, §v6.22 r30a).

| From r16 `dancin` | From r30a `triphop` | Meet in the middle |
|---|---|---|
| deep house, four-on-the-floor kick (now `soft`), rolling sub, chord stabs, electric piano, airy pads, the wordless vocal chop | `Bristol` (the British lever, in genre position), crackly vinyl breakbeat, minor piano loop, strings sample, melodica, the haunting low sung chorus, voices loud in front | **110 BPM** (between 120 and 90), both in **B-flat minor**, plus the **unity sentence** (`one piece of music, not a remix`) so it doesn't sound like one track pasted on the other |
| verse one `smooth and tight on the groove` · verse two `clipped BBC newsreader voice… cold and dry` | verse one `low, menacing, half-whispered` · verse two `quiet, clipped, cold` | both kept for each man |

**Exclude rebuilt:** r30a banned `house, four on the floor` and r16 banned `breakbeat`. Both **unbanned**, because each record's ban was the other record's core sound. Kept: the voice and accent guards, the other lanes' genres, `riser, crescendo` (voice ducking).
**Lyrics = r30a's** (r28 layout, sung chorus, new verse ⬜ still waiting for approval). Cues blend both grooves.
🆕 **Suno's own tool for this is v6 Mashup** ("combine elements from your different songs and describe how they should work together", suno-v6.md §5). `suno.mts` doesn't drive it, so it's offered to Jack as a manual click-path.
Settings: v6 · SI 70 · Variety Off · Max Mode ON · Male · Personalize off · no Voice · Auto · camping-Jack · Weirdness 30 and 40. Titles `camping-r33-dancintriphop-v6-w30` / `-w40`.

```

Verse two: a posh Englishman, a clipped BBC newsreader voice, quiet, cold and dry on the same groove. Then the two men trade bars. Moody, hypnotic and paranoid, a late-night club record, dark words over a smooth groove.

### r32 triphop-strings atom

Style:

```
Bristol trip hop at 90 BPM in B-flat minor, dark, slow and heavy: a thudding, crackly vinyl breakbeat, a deep rumbling sub bass, a minor-key piano loop, record scratches and a haunting melodica, with a subtle dark orchestral layer woven underneath, low cellos, long sustained strings and distant muted horns, felt more than heard, so the strings and the break are one piece of music, not a remix of one by the other. Smoky, wide and cavernous, but the voices dry, close and loud in front the whole way. A haunting low male singer sings an aching chorus. Verse one: an English working-class man, a low, menacing, half-whispered rap, close on the mic, tight on the beat, cold threat under every line, a small edge of aggression. Verse two: a posh Englishman, a quiet, clipped, cold voice, just as menacing. Then the two men trade bars in the dark. Moody, paranoid and serious, one steady groove start to finish, never building, never dropping.
```

Exclude styles:

```
American accent, American vocal, Southern drawl, US rap, trap, autotune, teenage voice, boyish voice, young MC, boy band, female lead vocal, sung verses, cheesy, comedic, novelty, parody, spoken word, tempo change, drum and bass, dubstep, EDM, big room, fast tempo, upbeat, happy, major key, bouncy, four on the floor, house, ska, brass section, synth-pop, rave, Jamaican accent, boom bap, jazz rap, lo-fi hip hop, build, riser, beat drop, crescendo, epic orchestral, soaring strings, trailer music, film score, choir, americana, country vocal, gospel, shouted
```

Lyrics:

```lyrics
[Intro | a slow heavy breakbeat, a dark sub and low cellos | haunting sung male voice, low, wordless]
Ooh ooh-ooh yeah
[mid-range rasp voice, talking quick over the hook, spoken]
Yeah this one's about camping not the fun kind no marshmallows
the kind where the tent's your address
and the bloke in the big black motor thinks you picked it
Right let it drop
[Verse 1 | the heavy breakbeat thuds in | mid-range rasp voice, low menacing half-whispered rap, tight on the beat, a small edge of aggression]

and you looking to the side in shame but why
now let me explain how I'm just poor
you keep on walking through that Wait trose door
presenting yourself with your shiny teeth
fucking sense of entitlement and self belief

but I bet that you paid for your wheels on tick
cash from the bank for your wank tank
four tonnes of steel just to get a meal deal
you got cheese but I want Cheddar I can't live like this forever

now I insist that I hold that door
please sir can I fuckin have some more?

still out here holding the door

ooh we don't have long

ooh we don't have long

[crisp posh voice]

back to that time when we very first met

yet I don't begrudge you it's us and them now

well we don't have long and by the time it hits we'll be gone

still out here holding the door

ooh we don't have long

## v6.23 Round r31 — `triphop-rage` (2026-09-16)

**Brief, Jack 2026-09-16, on `camping-r30a-triphop-v6-w30`:** *"this one is great but it needs more agression, the voice
needs to sound like way down we go by Kaleo, but keep it close to the original … also add a layer of subtle
orchestralness too it."* ⬜ Two takes have that title ([256b4cec](https://suno.com/song/256b4cec-f309-410a-b30f-f865c9670100) ·
[ec384b89](https://suno.com/song/ec384b89-f211-4215-b5fe-a3d5889226bd)) and which one Jack means isn't recorded. The prompt is the same either way.

**Deliberately meek (keep it close):** r30a's atom with three changes and nothing else.

| Ask | Change |
|---|---|
| Kaleo voice | no artist name. Research: *raspy, soulful, bluesy, a deep raspy growl, moving easily between the bottom and high registers, grit and distortion in the singing* ([Singing Carrots](https://singingcarrots.com/learn-to-sing-like/Kaleo), [Songfacts](https://www.songfacts.com/facts/kaleo/way-down-we-go)) → the chorus singer is now `a raw, raspy, soulful male singer belts an aching bluesy chorus, growling low and cracking up high`, and the verse voice is `raw, raspy, gritty` |
| more aggression | `low, menacing, half-whispered` → `a snarling rap… spitting with aggression`. Tarquin `quiet… cold` → `cold voice turning vicious, just as aggressive`. `angry` goes in the mood line. **`shouted` unbanned** (the belt needs room) |
| subtle orchestra | `an eerie strings sample` → `a subtle dark orchestral layer woven underneath, low cellos, long sustained strings and distant muted horns, felt more than heard`, plus the **unity sentence** (tag-mechanics) so it doesn't sound bolted on. Bans added: `epic orchestral, soaring strings, trailer music, film score, choir`. Swells aren't used, because r30a says `never building` |
| accent guard | Kaleo's blues pulls toward American blues voices (v6 rock drifts to country/southern, suno-v6.md §1), so `americana, country vocal, gospel` are banned. `Bristol` stays in genre position |

Settings = r30a's, but **Weirdness 30 and 20** (lower = closer to the prompt; the original was w30). v6 · SI 70 · Variety Off · Max Mode ON · Male · Personalize off · no Voice · Auto · camping-Jack.
Titles `camping-r31-triphoprage-v6-w30` / `-w20`.

**Generated 2026-09-16 (Jack's standing ask)**: 2 Creates made 4 takes at 20 credits each with Max Mode (5,170 → 5,130). The first attempts stopped before Create because the Style box was 1,006/1,000 characters (the loader caught the cut-off). `A smoky late-night atmosphere, wide and cavernous` was shortened to `Smoky, wide and cavernous`.

## v6.22 Round r30 — five bold lanes: trip hop · ska · Madchester · rave · synth-pop (2026-09-16)

**Brief, Jack 2026-09-16:** he picked five styles off the "new styles" list: *"Bristol trip-hop … Nutty ska / Madness-style …
Baggy / Madchester … Jersey club / UK rave revival … 80s synth-pop … please try these."* Each lane is a **bold** rewrite with its own Style box and its own Exclude box, and each bans the other lanes' sounds. **Lyrics = r28's** (comma-stripped, one cue per section, sung chorus, the new verse ⬜ still waiting for Jack's approval). Only the groove, rap and chorus words in the cues change per lane.

**Repo rules applied:**
- **Trip hop:** `trip hop` alone is a weak British word (suno-voices.md §4), so **`Bristol`** leads. No `skit` and no `dusty`, which are both US hip-hop words (§4c). Trip hop's build-and-drop habit ducks the voice (Thread 5 §5), so: `voices… loud in front the whole way`, `one steady groove… never building`, and `build, riser, beat drop, crescendo` banned.
- **Ska:** **`skank` is blocked by Suno's filter** (tag-mechanics), so the offbeat is described instead: `stabbing one short chord on every upbeat`. Offbeat plus brass drifts to oompah, so `oompah, brass band, marching band, dixieland, New Orleans` are banned, along with the ska/dub vocal defaults (`toasting, ragga MC, Jamaican accent`). `dancehall` stays out of the Style box because it's a Jamaican genre word.
- **Madchester:** the place name leads, **no `skit`** (the r11 lesson), and **no bans on the scene's own sound** (`indie rock`, `jangly guitars`, `band`). Banning those is what sent r11 American (§4a).
- **Rave:** Jersey club is an American (Newark) scene word, so **`UK rave` leads** and the Jersey club parts are described without naming it (`a bouncing syncopated five-hit kick pattern`, `bed-squeak percussion`). Chopped vocals are kept `in the gaps between the lines` so they don't drown the words.
- **Synth-pop:** no artist name. `dry, cool, arch, deadpan, half-spoken` gets the Pet Shop Boys feel, especially for Tarquin.

**Research** (read, not heard): trip hop is dark, atmospheric and breakbeat-led. 2-tone is fast 1980s British ska with a driving bass, offbeat organ/piano, a prominent brass section and London voices. Madchester is 105–130 BPM with jangly guitars, a funky bass and a shuffling beat. Jersey club is 130–140 BPM with a five-hit kick on 16ths 1-5-9-12-15, the bed squeak and chopped vocals. 80s synth-pop is analog synth bass, gated drums and arpeggios at 112–118 BPM. Sources: [roo.beehiiv prompt list](https://roo.beehiiv.com/p/complete-list-of-prompts-styles-for-suno-ai-music-2026), [MasterClass Madchester](https://www.masterclass.com/articles/madchester-music-guide), [DJ Mag Happy Mondays](https://djmag.com/features/how-happy-mondays-pills-n-thrills-and-bellyaches-became-pinnacle-madchester-sound), [Orphiq jersey club](https://orphiq.com/resources/what-is-jersey-club), [Beatstorapon jersey club](https://beatstorapon.com/blog/how-to-make-a-jersey-club-beat/), [HookGenius pop](https://hookgenius.app/learn/suno-pop-prompts/).

Settings for all lanes: v6 · Style Influence 70 · Variety Off · **Max Mode ON** · Male · Personalize off · no Voice · Duration Auto · camping-Jack · Weirdness 30 and 40. Titles `camping-r30<lane>-v6-w30/-w40`.

**Generated 2026-09-16 (Jack's standing ask)**: 10 Creates made 20 takes, 20 credits each with Max Mode. The form was checked before each Create.

## v6.21 Round r29 — `afro`: amapiano and afroswing, two bold lanes (2026-09-16)

**Brief, Jack 2026-09-16:** after 28 rounds, *"nothing is working"*. From the list of new styles he chose **"Afroswing /
amapiano: please try this one."** This is a bold rewrite: two separate lanes, each with its own Style and its own Exclude box, and each bans the other lane's sound.

**What the research says** (read, not heard):
- **Amapiano**: 108–118 BPM. The **log drum is the bass** (a tuned, woody, percussive sound), so don't write `kick drum` or heavy `808`/sub tags, which take over its role. Use jazzy / Rhodes piano (not a generic `piano`), shakers and congas, and a swung, off-grid groove. Don't mix it with `afro house`, which blurs the groove ([HookGenius amapiano](https://hookgenius.app/learn/suno-amapiano-prompts/), [Native Instruments](https://blog.native-instruments.com/amapiano-music/), [inspiredbybeatz](https://www.inspiredbybeatz.com/en/amapiano-production-how-the-log-drum-sound-is-created/)).
- **Afroswing**: UK, mid-2010s, from afrobeats and dancehall crossed with grime and UK rap. About 95–110 BPM. Off-beat shakers, syncopated kicks, claps landing on 3 or late, swung hats, a three-note pattern inside 4/4, a warm sub ([Wikipedia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Afroswing), [The Sound Atlas](https://thesoundatlas.org/discover/afroswing)).

**House-rule calls:**
- 🔴 **Accent:** amapiano is South African, so **`UK` leads the genre words** in both lanes, `English` / `London` is written into the voices, and `South African accent` / `Jamaican patois` / `Nigerian accent` are banned. Repo evidence says Exclude bans barely control nationality, and the genre word is what does it.
- 🔴 **Casting:** these genres come with young London MCs as their default voice, and our men are 47 and posh. `young MC, teenage voice, boyish voice` are banned, and the voice words are carried over from r27/r28.
- 🟡 **Pace:** both genres are slower than r27's 124 BPM. The fast rap is asked for as **double time over a slow groove** (amapiano) or **quick and conversational on the swing** (afroswing).
- **Kept from r28:** the comma-stripped lyrics, one cue per section, the sung chorus, and the new verse (⬜ still waiting for Jack's approval). Only the groove words in the cues change.

Settings for both lanes: v6 · Style Influence 70 · Variety Off · **Max Mode ON** · Male · Personalize off · no Voice · Duration Auto · camping-Jack · Weirdness 30 and 40.
Titles `camping-r29a-amapiano-v6-w30/-w40` · `camping-r29b-afroswing-v6-w30/-w40`.

**Generated 2026-09-16 (Jack's standing ask)**: 4 Creates made 8 takes at 20 credits each with Max Mode (5,490 → 5,410). The form was checked before each Create.

- **r29b afroswing** · w30: [4baee3c0](https://suno.com/song/4baee3c0-4dbb-450b-80e4-45c409419f2d) · [fe15555d](https://suno.com/song/fe15555d-8606-45b9-8da1-f8e3f962e525) · w40: [371aabd2](https://suno.com/song/371aabd2-f622-4d52-9993-3f3db611bf06) · [126e3843](https://suno.com/song/126e3843-29e2-44cd-a49a-e56bf959faa2)

Then the two men trade bars at the same fast pace. Warm, euphoric and celebratory, dark words over a sunny dancefloor groove.

## v6.20 Round r28 — `charts` (2026-09-16)

**Brief, Jack 2026-09-16, on r27 w40 (✅ "this works"):** *"do a new one with elements of this, add more rap,
like the fast part when he says 'you are intent on living in a tent / it's a lack of work ethic, it's
pathetic / getting parra lettic' … why when i say to do the whole song as fast as this part, that doesnt
happen, is there something in the lyrics prompts that mess it up … optimise it … use all of the tools to the
limit and make a song that belongs in the charts."*

### Why only the tent lines go fast — read from r27's lyrics box

Rule from `docs/suno-gpt/files/lyric-craft.md` "Punctuation is the brake": **Suno gives each lyric line
one musical phrase and squeezes that line's syllables into it. A comma is a beat of inserted space, and a
bracket cue starts a new mini-section.** No tempo word in the Style box can override that.

| | The fast lines (verse 2, lines 1–6) | Everywhere else |
|---|---|---|
| Commas | 0 · 2 (a rhyme pivot: `ethic, it's pathetic`) · 1 · 0 · 0 · 0 | Verse 1: **1–2 on nearly every line**, often in the first three words (`Once again,` · `now, let me explain, how`), so the line is chopped into 3 slow units |
| Syllables per line | 10 · 11 · **14** · 11 · 9 | Verse 1: 8–12. Endings: 5–6 (`it's us and them now`), which get stretched |
| Cues | **six lines in a row with no bracket** | Verse 1: **3 cues in 17 lines**; the breakdown: **a label every 2 lines**. Every cue resets the phrasing |
| Internal rhymes | dense (`intent/tent`, `ethic/pathetic/parra lettic`, `sorrow/tomorrow`, `exist/insist`) | mostly end-rhymes only |
| Arrangement | full groove, `straight in` | the kick `cuts out` and the bass `drops out`: the beat itself stops, so the rap waits |

Web research agrees: **lines over ~15 syllables get rushed, similar lengths across lines keep the rhythm, and fast
flow comes from dense internal and multi-syllable rhymes**
([HookGenius lyrics guide](https://hookgenius.app/learn/suno-custom-lyrics-guide/),
[HookGenius fast-flow](https://hookgenius.app/learn/suno-eminem-style/)). ⬜ This is a reading of the boxes, not
an A/B test. r28 changes all of these at once, so a win proves the whole package works, not which part did it.

### What r28 does

| Lever | Change |
|---|---|
| **Commas** | stripped from every rap line except rhyme pivots and the punchline question marks. **No word changed** |
| **Cues** | **one cue per section**, nothing mid-verse. The kick-cut and whispered drops are gone. The trade keeps the one-label-per-turn pattern only where the speaker changes |
| **Short lines** | merged where two short clauses belong together (`that you just stop the grift what about if we taxed the rich?` · `yet I don't begrudge you it's us and them now`). Line breaks only |
| **More rap** | the breakdown becomes a **full-groove rapped trade**, the 8-bar instrumental is gone, and there's 🆕 **a new 8-line verse** written dense (13–17 syllables, internal rhymes) in the fast part's style. It **names who benefits from the robots** (the-reader.md: never raise automation fear without naming who gains). ⬜ **New words, awaiting Jack's approval** |
| **Charts** | a **real sung chorus** with words (it was wordless), built from the song's own lines (`please sir can I have some more`, `holding that door`, `we don't have long`), 4 lines against 14–16-line verses (research: roughly 2:1), repeated three times. Structure: intro → V1 → chorus → V2 → chorus → V3 trade → V4 new → final trade → chorus → outro. Style box: `polished radio-ready club mix`, `a big catchy sung chorus`. `sung chorus` unbanned; `sung verses` stays banned so the rappers keep rapping |
| **The pace, in the Style box** | `rapid-fire and relentless … words packed tight with internal rhymes … that same fast pace from the first bar to the last` (density words, not a beat count). Bans added: `slow flow, relaxed flow, slow delivery, drawn-out words` |
| **Tools: Max Mode ON** 🆕 | Suno: *"uses more compute to maximize consistency throughout the song… recommended for songs over two minutes"*, which is exactly the problem of keeping one pace all the way through. **Costs 2× credits** ([Suno v6 release notes](https://suno.com/release-notes/introducing-v6), [roo.beehiiv](https://roo.beehiiv.com/p/suno-v6-features), [undetectr](https://undetectr.com/blog/suno-v6-changing-your-prompt)). Variety stays **Off**, because anything above Off lets Suno rewrite the Style box |

Kept from r27: 124 BPM deep house in B-flat minor, bounce, plucked hook, the mid-range light-rasp voice, the posh
second man, the small bite, every original word.
Settings: v6 · Style Influence 70 · Variety Off · **Max Mode ON** · Male · Personalize off · no Voice ·
Duration Auto · camping-Jack · Weirdness 30 and 40. Titles `camping-r28-charts-v6-w30` / `-w40`.

**Generated 2026-09-16 (Jack's standing ask)**: 2 Creates made 4 takes at **20 credits each with Max Mode** (5,530 → 5,490). The form was checked before each Create, with Max Mode read back as On.

[271e1f98](https://suno.com/song/271e1f98-1fdd-4016-9014-e6c5c6d42b81) ·
